**Technical Details:**
- **Method**: `LineageRunner` from `sqllineage.runner`
- **Input SQL**: `"INSERT INTO taba (a1, b1) VALUES (1,(SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb ));"`
- **Expected Result**: `source_tables` should include `tabb`
- **Actual Result**: `source_tables` is empty

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Import `LineageRunner` from `sqllineage.runner`
2. Create SQL statement with INSERT INTO ... VALUES containing a subquery
3. Run `LineageRunner` with dialect="ansi"
4. Check `analysis_result.source_tables` - it should detect the source table from subquery

### 1.5 Best Practices
- Understand existing parsing logic for VALUES clauses
- Ensure minimal changes to maintain compatibility
- Test various subquery patterns in VALUES
- Verify existing functionality isn't broken

     1	# SQLLineage
     2	SQL Lineage Analysis Tool powered by Python
     3	
     4	[![image](https://img.shields.io/pypi/v/sqllineage.svg)](https://pypi.org/project/sqllineage/)
     5	[![image](https://img.shields.io/pypi/status/sqllineage.svg)](https://pypi.org/project/sqllineage/)
     6	[![image](https://img.shields.io/pypi/pyversions/sqllineage.svg)](https://pypi.org/project/sqllineage/)
     7	[![image](https://img.shields.io/pypi/l/sqllineage.svg)](https://pypi.org/project/sqllineage/)
     8	[![Build Status](https://github.com/reata/sqllineage/workflows/build/badge.svg)](https://github.com/reata/sqllineage/actions)
     9	[![Documentation Status](https://readthedocs.org/projects/sqllineage/badge/?version=latest)](https://sqllineage.readthedocs.io/en/latest/?badge=latest)
    10	[![codecov](https://codecov.io/gh/reata/sqllineage/branch/master/graph/badge.svg)](https://codecov.io/gh/reata/sqllineage)
    11	[![Code style: black](https://img.shields.io/badge/code%20style-black-000000.svg)](https://github.com/psf/black)
    12	[![security: bandit](https://img.shields.io/badge/security-bandit-yellow.svg)](https://github.com/PyCQA/bandit)
    13	
    14	Never get the hang of a SQL parser? SQLLineage comes to the rescue. Given a SQL command, SQLLineage will tell you its
    15	source and target tables, without worrying about Tokens, Keyword, Identifier and all the jagons used by SQL parsers.
    16	
    17	Behind the scene, SQLLineage pluggable leverages parser library ([`sqlfluff`](https://github.com/sqlfluff/sqlfluff) 
    18	and [`sqlparse`](https://github.com/andialbrecht/sqlparse)) to parse the SQL command, analyze the AST, stores the lineage
    19	information in a graph (using graph library [`networkx`](https://github.com/networkx/networkx)), and brings you all the 
    20	human-readable result with ease.
    21	
    22	## Demo & Documentation
    23	Talk is cheap, show me a [demo](https://reata.github.io/sqllineage/).
    24	
    25	[Documentation](https://sqllineage.readthedocs.io) is online hosted by readthedocs, and you can check the 
    26	[release note](https://sqllineage.readthedocs.io/en/latest/release_note/changelog.html) there.
    27	
    28	
    29	## Quick Start
    30	Install sqllineage via PyPI:
    31	```bash
    32	$ pip install sqllineage
    33	```
    34	
    35	Using sqllineage command to parse a quoted-query-string:
    36	```
    37	$ sqllineage -e "insert into db1.table1 select * from db2.table2"
    38	Statements(#): 1
    39	Source Tables:
    40	    db2.table2
    41	Target Tables:
    42	    db1.table1
    43	```
    44	
    45	Or you can parse a SQL file with -f option:
    46	```
    47	$ sqllineage -f foo.sql
    48	Statements(#): 1
    49	Source Tables:
    50	    db1.table_foo
    51	    db1.table_bar
    52	Target Tables:
    53	    db2.table_baz
    54	```
    55	
    56	## Advanced Usage
    57	
    58	### Multiple SQL Statements
    59	Lineage result combined for multiple SQL statements, with intermediate tables identified:
    60	```
    61	$ sqllineage -e "insert into db1.table1 select * from db2.table2; insert into db3.table3 select * from db1.table1;"
    62	Statements(#): 2
    63	Source Tables:
    64	    db2.table2
    65	Target Tables:
    66	    db3.table3
    67	Intermediate Tables:
    68	    db1.table1
    69	```
    70	
    71	### Verbose Lineage Result
    72	And if you want to see lineage result for every SQL statement, just toggle verbose option
    73	```
    74	$ sqllineage -v -e "insert into db1.table1 select * from db2.table2; insert into db3.table3 select * from db1.table1;"
    75	Statement #1: insert into db1.table1 select * from db2.table2;
    76	    table read: [Table: db2.table2]
    77	    table write: [Table: db1.table1]
    78	    table cte: []
    79	    table rename: []
    80	    table drop: []
    81	Statement #2: insert into db3.table3 select * from db1.table1;
    82	    table read: [Table: db1.table1]
    83	    table write: [Table: db3.table3]
    84	    table cte: []
    85	    table rename: []
    86	    table drop: []
    87	==========
    88	Summary:
    89	Statements(#): 2
    90	Source Tables:
    91	    db2.table2
    92	Target Tables:
    93	    db3.table3
    94	Intermediate Tables:
    95	    db1.table1
    96	```
    97	
    98	### Dialect-Awareness Lineage
    99	By default, sqllineage doesn't validate your SQL and could give confusing result in case of invalid SQL syntax.
   100	In addition, different SQL dialect has different set of keywords, further weakening sqllineage's capabilities when 
   101	keyword used as table name or column name. To reduce the impact, user are strongly encouraged to pass the dialect to 
   102	assist the lineage analyzing. 
   103	
   104	Take below example, `analyze` is a reserved keyword in PostgreSQL. Default non-validating dialect gives incomplete result,
   105	while ansi dialect gives the correct one and postgres dialect tells you this causes syntax error:
   106	```
   107	$ sqllineage -e "insert into analyze select * from foo;"
   108	Statements(#): 1
   109	Source Tables:
   110	    <default>.foo
   111	Target Tables:
   112	    
   113	$ sqllineage -e "insert into analyze select * from foo;" --dialect=ansi
   114	Statements(#): 1
   115	Source Tables:
   116	    <default>.foo
   117	Target Tables:
   118	    <default>.analyze
   119	
   120	$ sqllineage -e "insert into analyze select * from foo;" --dialect=postgres
   121	...
   122	sqllineage.exceptions.InvalidSyntaxException: This SQL statement is unparsable, please check potential syntax error for SQL
   123	```
   124	
   125	Use `sqllineage --dialects` to see all available dialects.
   126	
   127	### Column-Level Lineage
   128	We also support column level lineage in command line interface, set level option to column, all column lineage path will 
   129	be printed.
   130	
   131	```sql
   132	INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE foo
   133	SELECT a.col1,
   134	       b.col1     AS col2,
   135	       c.col3_sum AS col3,
   136	       col4,
   137	       d.*
   138	FROM bar a
   139	         JOIN baz b
   140	              ON a.id = b.bar_id
   141	         LEFT JOIN (SELECT bar_id, sum(col3) AS col3_sum
   142	                    FROM qux
   143	                    GROUP BY bar_id) c
   144	                   ON a.id = sq.bar_id
   145	         CROSS JOIN quux d;
   146	
   147	INSERT OVERWRITE TABLE corge
   148	SELECT a.col1,
   149	       a.col2 + b.col2 AS col2
   150	FROM foo a
   151	         LEFT JOIN grault b
   152	              ON a.col1 = b.col1;
   153	```
   154	
   155	Suppose this sql is stored in a file called foo.sql
   156	
   157	```
   158	$ sqllineage -f foo.sql -l column
   159	<default>.corge.col1 <- <default>.foo.col1 <- <default>.bar.col1
   160	<default>.corge.col2 <- <default>.foo.col2 <- <default>.baz.col1
   161	<default>.corge.col2 <- <default>.grault.col2
   162	<default>.foo.* <- <default>.quux.*
   163	<default>.foo.col3 <- c.col3_sum <- <default>.qux.col3
   164	<default>.foo.col4 <- col4
   165	```
   166	
   167	### Lineage Visualization
   168	One more cool feature, if you want a graph visualization for the lineage result, toggle graph-visualization option
   169	
   170	Still using the above SQL file
   171	```
   172	sqllineage -g -f foo.sql
   173	```
   174	A webserver will be started, showing DAG representation of the lineage result in browser:
   175	
   176	- Table-Level Lineage
   177	
   178	<img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/reata/sqllineage/master/docs/_static/table.jpg" alt="Table-Level Lineage">
   179	
   180	- Column-Level Lineage
   181	
   182	<img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/reata/sqllineage/master/docs/_static/column.jpg" alt="Column-Level Lineage">
   183

     3	from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

     6	from sqllineage.core.holders import SQLLineageHolder

    18	def lazy_method(func):
    19	    def wrapper(*args, **kwargs):
    20	        self = args[0]
    21	        if not self._evaluated:
    22	            self._eval()
    23	        return func(*args, **kwargs)
    24	
    25	    return wrapper
    26	
    27	
    28	def lazy_property(func):
    29	    return property(lazy_method(func))
    30	
    31	
    32	class LineageRunner(object):
    33	    def __init__(
    34	        self,
    35	        sql: str,
    36	        dialect: str = DEFAULT_DIALECT,
    37	        encoding: Optional[str] = None,
    38	        verbose: bool = False,
    39	        draw_options: Optional[Dict[str, str]] = None,
    40	    ):
    41	        """
    42	        The entry point of SQLLineage after command line options are parsed.
    43	
    44	        :param sql: a string representation of SQL statements.
    45	        :param encoding: the encoding for sql string
    46	        :param verbose: verbose flag indicate whether statement-wise lineage result will be shown
    47	        """
    48	        if dialect == SQLPARSE_DIALECT:
    49	            warnings.warn(
    50	                "dialect `non-validating` is deprecated, use `ansi` or dialect of your SQL instead. "
    51	                "`non-validating` will stop being the default dialect in v1.5.x release "
    52	                "and be completely removed in v1.6.x",
    53	                DeprecationWarning,
    54	                stacklevel=2,
    55	            )
    56	        self._encoding = encoding
    57	        self._sql = sql
    58	        self._verbose = verbose
    59	        self._draw_options = draw_options if draw_options else {}
    60	        self._evaluated = False
    61	        self._stmt: List[str] = []
    62	        self._dialect = dialect
    63	
    64	    @lazy_method
    65	    def __str__(self):
    66	        """
    67	        print out the Lineage Summary.
    68	        """
    69	        statements = self.statements()
    70	        source_tables = "\n    ".join(str(t) for t in self.source_tables)
    71	        target_tables = "\n    ".join(str(t) for t in self.target_tables)
    72	        combined = f"""Statements(#): {len(statements)}
    73	Source Tables:
    74	    {source_tables}
    75	Target Tables:
    76	    {target_tables}
    77	"""
    78	        if self.intermediate_tables:
    79	            intermediate_tables = "\n    ".join(
    80	                str(t) for t in self.intermediate_tables
    81	            )
    82	            combined += f"""Intermediate Tables:
    83	    {intermediate_tables}"""
    84	        if self._verbose:
    85	            result = ""
    86	            for i, holder in enumerate(self._stmt_holders):
    87	                stmt_short = statements[i].replace("\n", "")
    88	                if len(stmt_short) > 50:
    89	                    stmt_short = stmt_short[:50] + "..."
    90	                content = str(holder).replace("\n", "\n    ")
    91	                result += f"""Statement #{i + 1}: {stmt_short}
    92	    {content}
    93	"""
    94	            combined = result + "==========\nSummary:\n" + combined
    95	        return combined
    96	
    97	    @lazy_method
    98	    def to_cytoscape(self, level=LineageLevel.TABLE) -> List[Dict[str, Dict[str, str]]]:
    99	        """
   100	        to turn the DAG into cytoscape format.
   101	        """
   102	        if level == LineageLevel.COLUMN:
   103	            return to_cytoscape(self._sql_holder.column_lineage_graph, compound=True)
   104	        else:
   105	            return to_cytoscape(self._sql_holder.table_lineage_graph)
   106	
   107	    def draw(self, dialect: str) -> None:
   108	        """
   109	        to draw the lineage directed graph
   110	        """
   111	        draw_options = self._draw_options
   112	        if draw_options.get("f") is None:
   113	            draw_options.pop("f", None)
   114	            draw_options["e"] = self._sql
   115	            draw_options["dialect"] = dialect
   116	        return draw_lineage_graph(**draw_options)
   117	
   118	    @lazy_method
   119	    def statements(self) -> List[str]:
   120	        """
   121	        a list of SQL statements.
   122	        """
   123	        return [trim_comment(s) for s in self._stmt]
   124	
   125	    @lazy_property
   126	    def source_tables(self) -> List[Table]:
   127	        """
   128	        a list of source :class:`sqllineage.models.Table`
   129	        """
   130	        return sorted(self._sql_holder.source_tables, key=lambda x: str(x))
   131	
   132	    @lazy_property
   133	    def target_tables(self) -> List[Table]:
   134	        """
   135	        a list of target :class:`sqllineage.models.Table`
   136	        """
   137	        return sorted(self._sql_holder.target_tables, key=lambda x: str(x))
   138	
   139	    @lazy_property
   140	    def intermediate_tables(self) -> List[Table]:
   141	        """
   142	        a list of intermediate :class:`sqllineage.models.Table`
   143	        """
   144	        return sorted(self._sql_holder.intermediate_tables, key=lambda x: str(x))
   145	
   146	    @lazy_method
   147	    def get_column_lineage(self, exclude_subquery=True) -> List[Tuple[Column, Column]]:
   148	        """
   149	        a list of column tuple :class:`sqllineage.models.Column`
   150	        """
   151	        # sort by target column, and then source column
   152	        return sorted(
   153	            self._sql_holder.get_column_lineage(exclude_subquery),
   154	            key=lambda x: (str(x[-1]), str(x[0])),
   155	        )
   156	
   157	    def print_column_lineage(self) -> None:
   158	        """
   159	        print column level lineage to stdout
   160	        """
   161	        for path in self.get_column_lineage():
   162	            print(" <- ".join(str(col) for col in reversed(path)))
   163	
   164	    def print_table_lineage(self) -> None:
   165	        """
   166	        print table level lineage to stdout
   167	        """
   168	        print(str(self))
   169	
   170	    def _eval(self):
   171	        self._stmt = split(self._sql.strip())
   172	        analyzer = (
   173	            SqlParseLineageAnalyzer()
   174	            if self._dialect == SQLPARSE_DIALECT
   175	            else SqlFluffLineageAnalyzer(self._dialect)
   176	        )
   177	        self._stmt_holders = [analyzer.analyze(stmt) for stmt in self._stmt]
   178	        self._sql_holder = SQLLineageHolder.of(*self._stmt_holders)
   179	        self._evaluated = True
   180

     1	import warnings
     2	
     3	from sqlfluff.core import Linter, SQLLexError, SQLParseError
     4	
     5	from sqllineage.core.analyzer import LineageAnalyzer
     6	from sqllineage.core.holders import StatementLineageHolder
     7	from sqllineage.core.parser.sqlfluff.extractors.base import BaseExtractor
     8	from sqllineage.exceptions import (
     9	    InvalidSyntaxException,
    10	    UnsupportedStatementException,
    11	)
    12	from sqllineage.utils.entities import AnalyzerContext
    13	
    14	
    15	class SqlFluffLineageAnalyzer(LineageAnalyzer):
    16	    """SQL Statement Level Lineage Analyzer for `sqlfluff`"""
    17	
    18	    def __init__(self, dialect: str):
    19	        self._dialect = dialect
    20	
    21	    def analyze(self, sql: str) -> StatementLineageHolder:
    22	        parsed = Linter(dialect=self._dialect).parse_string(sql)
    23	        violations = [
    24	            str(e)
    25	            for e in parsed.violations
    26	            if isinstance(e, (SQLLexError, SQLParseError))
    27	        ]
    28	        if violations:
    29	            violation_msg = "\n".join(violations)
    30	            raise InvalidSyntaxException(
    31	                f"This SQL statement is unparsable, please check potential syntax error for SQL:\n"
    32	                f"{sql}\n"
    33	                f"{violation_msg}"
    34	            )
    35	
    36	        statement_segment = None
    37	        for top_segment in getattr(parsed.tree, "segments", []):
    38	            if top_segment.type == "statement":
    39	                statement_segment = top_segment.segments[0]
    40	                break
    41	            elif top_segment.type == "batch":
    42	                statements = top_segment.get_children("statement")
    43	                if len(statements) > 1:
    44	                    warnings.warn(
    45	                        "SQL statements is not split by semicolon. "
    46	                        "SQLLineage is not guaranteed to generate correct result under this circumstances.",
    47	                        SyntaxWarning,
    48	                        stacklevel=2,
    49	                    )
    50	                statement_segment = statements[0].segments[0]
    51	                break
    52	        if statement_segment is None:
    53	            raise UnsupportedStatementException(
    54	                f"SQLLineage cannot parse SQL:" f"{sql}"
    55	            )  # pragma: no cover
    56	        else:
    57	            for extractor in [
    58	                extractor_cls(self._dialect)
    59	                for extractor_cls in BaseExtractor.__subclasses__()
    60	            ]:
    61	                if extractor.can_extract(statement_segment.type):
    62	                    lineage_holder = extractor.extract(
    63	                        statement_segment, AnalyzerContext()
    64	                    )
    65	                    return StatementLineageHolder.of(lineage_holder)
    66	            else:
    67	                raise UnsupportedStatementException(
    68	                    f"SQLLineage doesn't support analyzing statement type [{statement_segment.type}] for SQL:"
    69	                    f"{sql}"
    70	                )
    71

     1	from sqlfluff.core.parser import BaseSegment
     2	
     3	from sqllineage.core.holders import SubQueryLineageHolder
     4	from sqllineage.core.models import Path
     5	from sqllineage.core.parser.sqlfluff.extractors.base import BaseExtractor
     6	from sqllineage.core.parser.sqlfluff.extractors.select import SelectExtractor
     7	from sqllineage.core.parser.sqlfluff.models import SqlFluffColumn, SqlFluffTable
     8	from sqllineage.core.parser.sqlfluff.utils import (
     9	    get_child,
    10	    is_set_expression,
    11	    list_child_segments,
    12	)
    13	from sqllineage.utils.entities import AnalyzerContext
    14	from sqllineage.utils.helpers import escape_identifier_name
    15	
    16	
    17	class CreateInsertExtractor(BaseExtractor):
    18	    """
    19	    Create statement and Insert statement lineage extractor
    20	    """
    21	
    22	    SUPPORTED_STMT_TYPES = [
    23	        "create_table_statement",
    24	        "create_table_as_statement",
    25	        "create_view_statement",
    26	        "insert_statement",
    27	        "insert_overwrite_directory_hive_fmt_statement",
    28	    ]
    29	
    30	    def extract(
    31	        self,
    32	        statement: BaseSegment,
    33	        context: AnalyzerContext,
    34	    ) -> SubQueryLineageHolder:
    35	        holder = self._init_holder(context)
    36	        src_flag = tgt_flag = False
    37	        for segment in list_child_segments(statement):
    38	            if segment.type == "with_compound_statement":
    39	                holder |= self.delegate_to_cte(segment, holder)
    40	            elif segment.type == "bracketed" and any(
    41	                s.type == "with_compound_statement" for s in segment.segments
    42	            ):
    43	                for sgmt in segment.segments:
    44	                    if sgmt.type == "with_compound_statement":
    45	                        holder |= self.delegate_to_cte(segment, holder)
    46	            elif segment.type in ("select_statement", "set_expression"):
    47	                holder |= self.delegate_to_select(segment, holder)
    48	            elif segment.type == "bracketed" and (
    49	                self.list_subquery(segment) or is_set_expression(segment)
    50	            ):
    51	                # note regular subquery within SELECT statement is handled by SelectExtractor, this is only to handle
    52	                # top-level subquery in DML like: 1) create table foo as (subquery); 2) insert into foo (subquery)
    53	                # subquery here isn't added as read source, and it inherits DML-level write_columns if parsed
    54	                if subquery_segment := get_child(
    55	                    segment, "select_statement", "set_expression"
    56	                ):
    57	                    holder |= self.delegate_to_select(subquery_segment, holder)
    58	
    59	            elif segment.type == "bracketed":
    60	                # In case of bracketed column reference, add these target columns to holder
    61	                # so that when we compute the column level lineage
    62	                # we keep these columns into consideration
    63	                sub_segments = list_child_segments(segment)
    64	                if all(
    65	                    sub_segment.type in ["column_reference", "column_definition"]
    66	                    for sub_segment in sub_segments
    67	                ):
    68	                    # target columns only apply to bracketed column_reference and column_definition
    69	                    columns = []
    70	                    for sub_segment in sub_segments:
    71	                        if sub_segment.type == "column_definition":
    72	                            sub_segment = get_child(sub_segment, "identifier")
    73	                        columns.append(SqlFluffColumn.of(sub_segment))
    74	                    holder.add_write_column(*columns)
    75	
    76	            elif segment.type == "keyword":
    77	                if segment.raw_upper in [
    78	                    "INTO",
    79	                    "OVERWRITE",
    80	                    "TABLE",
    81	                    "VIEW",
    82	                    "DIRECTORY",
    83	                ] or (
    84	                    tgt_flag is True and segment.raw_upper in ["IF", "NOT", "EXISTS"]
    85	                ):
    86	                    tgt_flag = True
    87	                elif segment.raw_upper in ["LIKE", "CLONE"]:
    88	                    src_flag = True
    89	                continue
    90	
    91	            if tgt_flag:
    92	                if segment.type in ["table_reference", "object_reference"]:
    93	                    write_obj = SqlFluffTable.of(segment)
    94	                    holder.add_write(write_obj)
    95	                elif segment.type == "literal":
    96	                    if segment.raw.isnumeric():
    97	                        # Special Handling for Spark Bucket Table DDL
    98	                        pass
    99	                    else:
   100	                        holder.add_write(Path(escape_identifier_name(segment.raw)))
   101	                tgt_flag = False
   102	            if src_flag:
   103	                if segment.type in ["table_reference", "object_reference"]:
   104	                    holder.add_read(SqlFluffTable.of(segment))
   105	                src_flag = False
   106	        return holder
   107	
   108	    def delegate_to_cte(
   109	        self, segment: BaseSegment, holder: SubQueryLineageHolder
   110	    ) -> SubQueryLineageHolder:
   111	        from .cte import CteExtractor
   112	
   113	        return self.delegate_to(
   114	            CteExtractor, segment, AnalyzerContext(cte=holder.cte, write=holder.write)
   115	        )
   116	
   117	    def delegate_to_select(
   118	        self,
   119	        segment: BaseSegment,
   120	        holder: SubQueryLineageHolder,
   121	    ) -> SubQueryLineageHolder:
   122	        return self.delegate_to(
   123	            SelectExtractor,
   124	            segment,
   125	            AnalyzerContext(
   126	                cte=holder.cte,
   127	                write=holder.write,
   128	                write_columns=holder.write_columns,
   129	            ),
   130	        )

Let me look at how the parser processes the problematic SQL:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the CreateInsertExtractor, I can see that it handles INSERT statements. The issue seems to be that when we have VALUES with subqueries like:

INSERT INTO taba (a1, b1) VALUES (1,(SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb ));

The parser correctly identifies:
- Target table: taba (this works)
- But it doesn't identify the source table tabb from the subquery within VALUES

Looking at the CreateInsertExtractor.extract() method, I can see it processes segments and looks for:
1. with_compound_statement  
2. select_statement or set_expression
3. bracketed segments with subqueries
4. bracketed segments with column references
5. keywords like INTO, TABLE, etc.
6. table references

But the VALUES clause processing seems to be missing. Looking at lines 59-75, it handles bracketed segments that contain column references, but it doesn't seem to recursively process subqueries within those values.

The issue is that when we have VALUES (1, (SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb)), the subquery part (SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb) is not being processed to extract the source table tabb.

I need to understand how the VALUES clause is being parsed. Let me check if there's specific handling for VALUES keyword or if it's being treated as a generic bracketed segment.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me debug the actual parsing by creating a simple test to see what segments are generated:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/reata__sqllineage__1.4 && python -c "

## Phase 4. TEST CREATION: Creating a Reproduction Script<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/reata__sqllineage__1.4/test_reproduce_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Script to reproduce the issue with subqueries in VALUES clause
"""

import logging
from sqllineage.runner import LineageRunner

def test_basic_case():
    """Test the basic case from the issue description"""
    print("=== Testing Basic Case ===")
    verify_sql = "INSERT INTO taba (a1, b1) VALUES (1,(SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb ));"
    analysis_result = LineageRunner(sql=verify_sql, dialect="ansi")
    
    print(f"SQL: {verify_sql}")
    print(f"source_tables: {analysis_result.source_tables}")
    print(f"target_tables: {analysis_result.target_tables}")
    
    # Expected: source_tables should contain tabb, target_tables should contain taba
    expected_source = 1  # tabb
    expected_target = 1  # taba
    
    actual_source = len(analysis_result.source_tables)
    actual_target = len(analysis_result.target_tables)
    
    print(f"Expected source tables: {expected_source}, Actual: {actual_source}")
    print(f"Expected target tables: {expected_target}, Actual: {actual_target}")
    
    success = (actual_source == expected_source and actual_target == expected_target and 
               str(analysis_result.source_tables[0]) == "<default>.tabb")
    
    print(f"Test {'PASSED' if success else 'FAILED'}")
    return success

def test_more_complex_cases():
    """Test more complex cases with subqueries in VALUES"""
    print("\n=== Testing More Complex Cases ===")
    
    test_cases = [
        # Multiple subqueries in VALUES
        {
            "sql": "INSERT INTO tab1 (col1, col2, col3) VALUES ((SELECT id FROM tab2), 'text', (SELECT count(*) FROM tab3));",
            "expected_sources": 2,  # tab2, tab3
            "expected_targets": 1,  # tab1
            "description": "Multiple subqueries in VALUES"
        },
        # Nested subqueries
        {
            "sql": "INSERT INTO tab1 (col1) VALUES ((SELECT max(id) FROM (SELECT id FROM tab2 WHERE active = 1) sub));",
            "expected_sources": 1,  # tab2
            "expected_targets": 1,  # tab1
            "description": "Nested subqueries in VALUES"
        },
        # Simple VALUES without subqueries (control test)
        {
            "sql": "INSERT INTO tab1 (col1, col2) VALUES (1, 'text');",
            "expected_sources": 0,
            "expected_targets": 1,  # tab1
            "description": "Simple VALUES without subqueries"
        }
    ]
    
    all_passed = True
    for i, case in enumerate(test_cases):
        print(f"\nTest {i+1}: {case['description']}")
        print(f"SQL: {case['sql']}")
        
        analysis_result = LineageRunner(sql=case['sql'], dialect="ansi")
        
        print(f"source_tables: {analysis_result.source_tables}")
        print(f"target_tables: {analysis_result.target_tables}")
        
        actual_sources = len(analysis_result.source_tables)
        actual_targets = len(analysis_result.target_tables)
        
        passed = (actual_sources == case['expected_sources'] and 
                 actual_targets == case['expected_targets'])
        
        print(f"Expected sources: {case['expected_sources']}, Actual: {actual_sources}")
        print(f"Expected targets: {case['expected_targets']}, Actual: {actual_targets}")
        print(f"Test {'PASSED' if passed else 'FAILED'}")
        
        if not passed:
            all_passed = False
            
    return all_passed

if __name__ == "__main__":
    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)
    
    print("Reproducing the subquery in VALUES clause issue...")
    
    basic_success = test_basic_case()
    complex_success = test_more_complex_cases()
    
    print(f"\n=== SUMMARY ===")
    print(f"Basic test: {'PASSED' if basic_success else 'FAILED'}")
    print(f"Complex tests: {'PASSED' if complex_success else 'FAILED'}")
    
    if not (basic_success and complex_success):
        print("Issue reproduced successfully!")
        exit(1)
    else:
        print("All tests passed - issue appears to be fixed!")

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Understanding the Problem and Solution<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I have a clear understanding of the problem:

**Problem Location**: In `/workspace/reata__sqllineage__1.4/sqllineage/core/parser/sqlfluff/extractors/create_insert.py`, the `CreateInsertExtractor.extract()` method.

**What the problem is**: When processing INSERT statements with VALUES clauses that contain subqueries, the parser correctly identifies:
- The target table (works fine)
- The VALUES clause structure
- Individual values like literals

However, it does NOT recursively process expressions within the VALUES clause that contain subqueries. Specifically, when we have:
`VALUES (1,(SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb ))`

The parser sees:
- A values_clause with a bracketed segment
- Inside that bracketed segment: a literal '1' and an expression '(SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb )'

But it doesn't process that expression to extract the source table 'tabb'.

**How the test reproduces the issue**: My test script shows that source_tables is empty when it should contain tabb.

**Best practices for the fix**:
1. Minimal change - only modify what's necessary
2. Recursively process expressions within VALUES clauses
3. Handle subqueries properly by delegating to SelectExtractor
4. Don't break existing functionality
5. Follow the existing pattern used elsewhere in the code

**How to fix the problem**:
Looking at the CreateInsertExtractor.extract() method, I need to add logic to handle expressions within VALUES clauses that contain subqueries.

Currently, the code processes:
1. bracketed segments looking for column references (lines 59-75)
2. But it doesn't look for expressions within those bracketed segments that contain subqueries

I need to add logic that:
1. When processing a bracketed segment in a values_clause
2. Look at each child segment
3. If a child is an expression that contains a subquery (select_statement or set_expression)
4. Delegate that subquery to SelectExtractor to process the source tables

Looking at the SQLFluff parse tree I generated earlier, I can see that within the values_clause.bracketed segment, there's an expression segment that contains a bracketed.select_statement. I need to detect and process this.

The fix should be in the bracketed segment processing part of CreateInsertExtractor.extract().</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement
The SQLLineage parser does not detect source tables from subqueries within VALUES clauses of INSERT statements.

### 5.2 Problem Location  
In `/workspace/reata__sqllineage__1.4/sqllineage/core/parser/sqlfluff/extractors/create_insert.py`, the `CreateInsertExtractor.extract()` method does not recursively process expressions within VALUES clauses that contain subqueries.

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue
My test script shows that for the SQL `INSERT INTO taba (a1, b1) VALUES (1,(SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb ));`, the `source_tables` result is empty when it should contain `tabb`.

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix
- Make minimal, focused changes
- Follow existing patterns in the codebase
- Recursively process nested structures
- Maintain backward compatibility
- Handle edge cases properly

### 5.5 How to Fix the Problem
Add logic in `CreateInsertExtractor.extract()` to detect and process expressions within VALUES clauses that contain subqueries by:
1. Examining child segments of bracketed segments in VALUES clauses
2. Identifying expressions that contain select statements
3. Delegating such subqueries to `SelectExtractor` for proper source table extraction

     1	"""
     2	Utils class to deal with the sqlfluff segments manipulations
     3	
     4	naming convention:
     5	    is_x: BaseSegment -> bool
     6	    find_x: BaseSegment -> Optional[BaseSegment]
     7	    list_x: BaseSegment -> List[BaseSegment]
     8	    extract_x: other pattern
     9	first parameter of each function must be sqlfluff BaseSegment
    10	"""
    11	from typing import List, Optional, Tuple
    12	
    13	from sqlfluff.core.parser import BaseSegment
    14	
    15	from sqllineage.utils.entities import ColumnQualifierTuple, SubQueryTuple
    16	
    17	
    18	def is_negligible(segment: BaseSegment) -> bool:
    19	    return (
    20	        segment.is_whitespace
    21	        or segment.is_comment
    22	        or bool(segment.is_meta)
    23	        or (segment.type == "symbol" and segment.raw != "*")
    24	    )
    25	
    26	
    27	def is_set_expression(segment: BaseSegment) -> bool:
    28	    return segment.type == "set_expression" or any(
    29	        seg.type == "set_expression" for seg in segment.segments
    30	    )
    31	
    32	
    33	def is_subquery(segment: BaseSegment) -> bool:
    34	    if segment.type == "from_expression_element" or segment.type == "bracketed":
    35	        token = extract_innermost_bracketed(
    36	            segment if segment.type == "bracketed" else segment.segments[0]
    37	        )
    38	        # check if innermost parenthesis contains SELECT
    39	        sub_token = (
    40	            token.get_child("select_statement")
    41	            or token.get_child("set_expression")
    42	            or (
    43	                token.get_child("expression")
    44	                and token.get_child("expression").get_child("select_statement")
    45	            )
    46	        )
    47	        if sub_token is not None:
    48	            return True
    49	    return False
    50	
    51	
    52	def is_wildcard(segment: BaseSegment) -> bool:
    53	    return segment.type == "wildcard_expression" or (
    54	        segment.type == "symbol" and segment.raw == "*"
    55	    )
    56	
    57	
    58	def find_from_expression_element(segment: BaseSegment) -> Optional[BaseSegment]:
    59	    """
    60	    segment can be of type:
    61	        from_clause as grandparent
    62	        from_expression/join_clause as parent
    63	    """
    64	    from_expression_element = None
    65	    if segment.type == "from_clause":
    66	        if from_expression := segment.get_child("from_expression"):
    67	            non_bracket = from_expression
    68	            while non_bracket.get_child("bracketed"):
    69	                non_bracket = non_bracket.get_child("bracketed")
    70	            if seg := non_bracket.get_child("from_expression_element"):
    71	                from_expression_element = seg
    72	            elif seg := non_bracket.get_child("from_expression"):
    73	                if sub_seg := seg.get_child("from_expression_element"):
    74	                    from_expression_element = sub_seg
    75	    elif segment.type in ("from_expression", "join_clause"):
    76	        if seg := segment.get_child("from_expression_element"):
    77	            from_expression_element = seg
    78	    return from_expression_element
    79	
    80	
    81	def find_table_identifier(segment: BaseSegment) -> Optional[BaseSegment]:
    82	    """
    83	    recursively find table identifier
    84	    """
    85	    table_identifier = None
    86	    if segment.type in ["table_reference", "file_reference", "object_reference"]:
    87	        return segment
    88	    else:
    89	        for sub_segment in segment.segments:
    90	            if identifier := find_table_identifier(sub_segment):
    91	                return identifier
    92	    return table_identifier
    93	
    94	
    95	def list_join_clause(segment: BaseSegment) -> List[BaseSegment]:
    96	    """
    97	    traverse from_clause, recursively goes into bracket by default
    98	    """
    99	    if from_expression := segment.get_child("from_expression"):
   100	        if bracketed := from_expression.get_child("bracketed"):
   101	            join_clauses = bracketed.get_children("join_clause")
   102	            if inner_bracket := bracketed.get_child("bracketed"):
   103	                join_clauses = list_join_clause(inner_bracket) + join_clauses
   104	            return join_clauses
   105	        else:
   106	            return from_expression.get_children("join_clause")
   107	    return []
   108	
   109	
   110	def list_expression_from_when_clause(
   111	    when_clause: BaseSegment, sub_type: str
   112	) -> List[BaseSegment]:
   113	    """
   114	    sub_type can be either WHEN or THEN, this is to extract subquery for case when (SELECT ...) then (SELECT ...)
   115	    """
   116	    segments = []
   117	    start_flag = False
   118	    from_keyword, end_keyword = sub_type, "THEN" if sub_type == "WHEN" else None
   119	    for segment in when_clause.segments:
   120	        if segment.type == "keyword" and segment.raw_upper == from_keyword:
   121	            start_flag = True
   122	            continue
   123	        if (
   124	            end_keyword is not None
   125	            and segment.type == "keyword"
   126	            and segment.raw_upper == end_keyword
   127	        ):
   128	            break
   129	        if start_flag and segment.type == "expression":
   130	            segments = segment.get_children("bracketed")
   131	    return segments
   132	
   133	
   134	def list_subqueries(segment: BaseSegment) -> List[SubQueryTuple]:
   135	    subquery = []
   136	    if segment.type == "select_clause":
   137	        as_segment = segment.get_child("select_clause_element").get_child(
   138	            "alias_expression"
   139	        )
   140	        select_clause = segment.get_child("select_clause_element")
   141	        case_expression = select_clause.get_child(
   142	            "expression"
   143	        ) and select_clause.get_child("expression").get_child("case_expression")
   144	        target = case_expression or select_clause.get_child("column_reference")
   145	        if target and target.type == "case_expression":
   146	            for when_clause in target.get_children("when_clause"):
   147	                for bracketed_segment in list_expression_from_when_clause(
   148	                    when_clause, sub_type="WHEN"
   149	                ):
   150	                    subquery.append(SubQueryTuple(bracketed_segment, None))
   151	                for bracketed_segment in list_expression_from_when_clause(
   152	                    when_clause, sub_type="THEN"
   153	                ):
   154	                    subquery.append(
   155	                        SubQueryTuple(bracketed_segment, extract_identifier(as_segment))
   156	                    )
   157	    elif segment.type == "from_expression_element":
   158	        as_segment, target = extract_as_and_target_segment(segment)
   159	        if is_subquery(target):
   160	            as_segment, target = extract_as_and_target_segment(segment)
   161	            subquery = [
   162	                SubQueryTuple(
   163	                    extract_innermost_bracketed(target)
   164	                    if not is_set_expression(target)
   165	                    else target,
   166	                    extract_identifier(as_segment) if as_segment else None,
   167	                )
   168	            ]
   169	    elif segment.type == "where_clause":
   170	        bracketeds = []
   171	        if expression := segment.get_child("expression"):
   172	            bracketeds = expression.get_children("bracketed")
   173	        elif bracketed_where := segment.get_child("bracketed"):
   174	            if expression := bracketed_where.get_child("expression"):
   175	                bracketeds = expression.get_children("bracketed")
   176	        subquery = [
   177	            SubQueryTuple(extract_innermost_bracketed(bracketed), None)
   178	            for bracketed in bracketeds
   179	            if is_subquery(bracketed)
   180	        ]
   181	    elif segment.type in ["from_clause", "from_expression"]:
   182	        if from_expression_element := find_from_expression_element(segment):
   183	            subquery = list_subqueries(from_expression_element)
   184	        for join_clause in list_join_clause(segment):
   185	            if from_expression_element := find_from_expression_element(join_clause):
   186	                subquery += list_subqueries(from_expression_element)
   187	    elif is_set_expression(segment):
   188	        subquery = [
   189	            SubQueryTuple(s, None)
   190	            for s in list_child_segments(segment, check_bracketed=True)
   191	            if s.type in ("bracketed", "select_statement")
   192	        ]
   193	    return subquery
   194	
   195	
   196	def list_child_segments(
   197	    segment: BaseSegment, check_bracketed: bool = True
   198	) -> List[BaseSegment]:
   199	    """
   200	    Filter segments for a given segment's children, recursive goes into bracket by default
   201	    """
   202	    if segment.type == "bracketed" and check_bracketed:
   203	        if is_set_expression(segment):
   204	            return [seg for seg in segment.segments if seg.type == "set_expression"]
   205	        else:
   206	            result = []
   207	            for seg in segment.iter_segments(
   208	                expanding=["expression"], pass_through=True
   209	            ):
   210	                if seg.type in ["column_reference", "column_definition"]:
   211	                    result.append(seg)
   212	                else:
   213	                    for s in seg.segments:
   214	                        if not is_negligible(s):
   215	                            result.append(s)
   216	            return result
   217	    else:
   218	        return [seg for seg in segment.segments if not is_negligible(seg)]
   219	
   220	
   221	def extract_identifier(col_segment: BaseSegment) -> str:
   222	    identifiers = list_child_segments(col_segment)
   223	    col_identifier = identifiers[-1]
   224	    return str(col_identifier.raw)
   225	
   226	
   227	def extract_as_and_target_segment(
   228	    segment: BaseSegment,
   229	) -> Tuple[BaseSegment, BaseSegment]:
   230	    as_segment = segment.get_child("alias_expression")
   231	    sublist = list_child_segments(segment, False)
   232	    target = sublist[0] if is_subquery(sublist[0]) else sublist[0].segments[0]
   233	    return as_segment, target
   234	
   235	
   236	def extract_column_qualifier(segment: BaseSegment) -> Optional[ColumnQualifierTuple]:
   237	    cqt = None
   238	    if is_wildcard(segment):
   239	        identifiers = segment.raw.split(".")
   240	        column = identifiers[-1]
   241	        parent = identifiers[-2] if len(identifiers) > 1 else None
   242	        cqt = ColumnQualifierTuple(column, parent)
   243	    elif segment.type == "column_reference":
   244	        sub_segments = list_child_segments(segment)
   245	        column = sub_segments[-1].raw
   246	        parent = sub_segments[-2].raw if len(sub_segments) > 1 else None
   247	        cqt = ColumnQualifierTuple(column, parent)
   248	    elif segment.type == "identifier":
   249	        cqt = ColumnQualifierTuple(segment.raw, None)
   250	    return cqt
   251	
   252	
   253	def extract_innermost_bracketed(bracketed_segment: BaseSegment) -> BaseSegment:
   254	    # in case of subquery in nested parenthesis like: `SELECT * FROM ((table))`, find the innermost one first
   255	    while True:
   256	        sub_bracketed_segments = [
   257	            bs.get_child("bracketed")
   258	            for bs in bracketed_segment.segments
   259	            if bs.get_child("bracketed")
   260	        ]
   261	        sub_paren = bracketed_segment.get_child("bracketed") or (
   262	            sub_bracketed_segments[0] if sub_bracketed_segments else None
   263	        )
   264	        if sub_paren is not None:
   265	            bracketed_segment = sub_paren
   266	        else:
   267	            break
   268	    return bracketed_segment
   269	
   270	
   271	def get_child(segment: BaseSegment, *seg_type: str) -> BaseSegment:
   272	    """
   273	    bypass mypy type check as sqlfluff get_child is untyped yet
   274	    """
   275	    return segment.get_child(*seg_type)
   276	
   277	
   278	def get_children(segment: BaseSegment, *seg_type: str) -> List[BaseSegment]:
   279	    """
   280	    bypass mypy type check as sqlfluff get_children is untyped yet
   281	    """
   282	    return segment.get_children(*seg_type)

"""
Debug script to understand segment structure for VALUES clause with subqueries
"""

import sqlfluff
from sqlfluff.core import Linter
from sqllineage.core.parser.sqlfluff.utils import list_child_segments

def debug_sql_structure():
    sql = "INSERT INTO taba (a1, b1) VALUES (1,(SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb ));"
    parsed = Linter(dialect='ansi').parse_string(sql)
    
    print("=== Full Parse Tree ===")
    def print_tree(segment, indent=0):
        prefix = "  " * indent
        print(f"{prefix}{segment.type}: '{segment.raw}'")
        if hasattr(segment, 'segments') and segment.segments:
            for child in segment.segments:
                print_tree(child, indent + 1)
    
    if parsed.tree and hasattr(parsed.tree, 'segments'):
        print_tree(parsed.tree)
    
    print("\n=== Focus on VALUES clause segments ===")
    # Find the values_clause and examine its structure
    def find_values_clause(segment):
        if segment.type == "values_clause":
            return segment
        if hasattr(segment, 'segments'):
            for child in segment.segments:
                result = find_values_clause(child)
                if result:
                    return result
        return None
    
    if parsed.tree:
        values_clause = find_values_clause(parsed.tree)
        if values_clause:
            print(f"Found values_clause: {values_clause.type}")
            print(f"Raw: '{values_clause.raw}'")
            
            # Look at bracketed children
            for child in values_clause.segments:
                if child.type == "bracketed":
                    print(f"Bracketed segment: '{child.raw}'")
                    sub_segments = list_child_segments(child)
                    print(f"Child segments ({len(sub_segments)}):")
                    for i, sub_seg in enumerate(sub_segments):
                        print(f"  {i}: {sub_seg.type}: '{sub_seg.raw}'")
                        if hasattr(sub_seg, 'segments') and sub_seg.segments:
                            for j, grandchild in enumerate(sub_seg.segments):
                                print(f"    {i}.{j}: {grandchild.type}: '{grandchild.raw}'")
                                if grandchild.type == "bracketed" and grandchild.segments:
                                    for k, ggchild in enumerate(grandchild.segments):
                                        print(f"      {i}.{j}.{k}: {ggchild.type}: '{ggchild.raw}'")

`(1,(SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb ))` which contains:
- literal: '1'  
- symbol: ','

Actually, let me first test what segments we actually get:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/reata__sqllineage__1.4/debug_segments2.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Debug script to understand what list_child_segments returns vs direct children
"""

import sqlfluff
from sqlfluff.core import Linter
from sqllineage.core.parser.sqlfluff.utils import list_child_segments, is_negligible

def debug_sql_structure():
    sql = "INSERT INTO taba (a1, b1) VALUES (1,(SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb ));"
    parsed = Linter(dialect='ansi').parse_string(sql)
    
    # Find the values_clause and examine its structure
    def find_values_clause(segment):
        if segment.type == "values_clause":
            return segment
        if hasattr(segment, 'segments'):
            for child in segment.segments:
                result = find_values_clause(child)
                if result:
                    return result
        return None
    
    if parsed.tree:
        values_clause = find_values_clause(parsed.tree)
        if values_clause:
            print(f"Found values_clause: {values_clause.type}")
            
            # Look at bracketed children
            for child in values_clause.segments:
                if child.type == "bracketed":
                    print(f"Bracketed segment: '{child.raw}'")
                    
                    # Compare list_child_segments vs direct children
                    sub_segments_filtered = list_child_segments(child)
                    direct_children = [seg for seg in child.segments if not is_negligible(seg)]
                    
                    print(f"list_child_segments result ({len(sub_segments_filtered)}):")
                    for i, sub_seg in enumerate(sub_segments_filtered):
                        print(f"  {i}: {sub_seg.type}: '{sub_seg.raw}'")
                        
                    print(f"Direct non-negligible children ({len(direct_children)}):")
                    for i, sub_seg in enumerate(direct_children):
                        print(f"  {i}: {sub_seg.type}: '{sub_seg.raw}'")
                        if hasattr(sub_seg, 'segments') and sub_seg.segments:
                            for j, grandchild in enumerate(sub_seg.segments):
                                print(f"    {i}.{j}: {grandchild.type}: '{grandchild.raw}'")

if __name__ == "__main__":

import sqlfluff
from sqlfluff.core import Linter

sql = 'INSERT INTO taba (a1, b1) VALUES (1,(SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb ));'
parsed = Linter(dialect='ansi').parse_string(sql)

def find_bracketed_segments(segment, results=None):
    if results is None:
        results = []
    if segment.type == 'bracketed':
        results.append(segment)
    if hasattr(segment, 'segments'):
        for child in segment.segments:
            find_bracketed_segments(child, results)
    return results

if parsed.tree:
    bracketed_segments = find_bracketed_segments(parsed.tree)
    print(f'Found {len(bracketed_segments)} bracketed segments')
    for i, seg in enumerate(bracketed_segments):
        print(f'  {i}: \"{seg.raw}\"')
        print(f'     Type: {seg.type}')
        print(f'     Has get_parent: {hasattr(seg, \"get_parent\")}')
        if hasattr(seg, 'segments') and seg.segments:
            print(f'     First child type: {seg.segments[0].type}')

import sqlfluff
from sqlfluff.core import Linter
sql = 'INSERT INTO taba (a1, b1) VALUES (1,(SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb ));'
parsed = Linter(dialect='ansi').parse_string(sql)
def find_bracketed_segments(segment, results=None):
    if results is None:
        results = []
    if segment.type == 'bracketed':
        results.append(segment)
    if hasattr(segment, 'segments'):
        for child in segment.segments:
            find_bracketed_segments(child, results)
    return results

    bracketed_segments = find_bracketed_segments(parsed.tree)

                        columns.append(SqlFluffColumn.of(sub_segment))
                    holder.add_write_column(*columns)
                else:

                    # Process expressions within VALUES clause that might contain subqueries

                # top-level subquery in DML like: 1) create table foo as (subquery); 2) insert into foo (subquery)
                # subquery here isn't added as read source, and it inherits DML-level write_columns if parsed

Let me check what's inside that expression:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/reata__sqllineage__1.4/debug_expression.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Debug script to understand what's inside the expression
"""

import sqlfluff
from sqlfluff.core import Linter
from sqllineage.core.parser.sqlfluff.utils import get_child

def debug_expression_contents():
    sql = "INSERT INTO taba (a1, b1) VALUES (1,(SELECT max(bb) FROM tabb ));"
    parsed = Linter(dialect='ansi').parse_string(sql)
    
    # Find the values_clause and the expression within it
    def find_values_expression(segment):
        if segment.type == "values_clause":
            # Look for bracketed -> expression
            for child in segment.segments:
                if child.type == "bracketed":
                    for subchild in child.segments:
                        if subchild.type == "expression":
                            return subchild
        if hasattr(segment, 'segments'):
            for child in segment.segments:
                result = find_values_expression(child)
                if result:
                    return result
        return None
    
    if parsed.tree:
        expression = find_values_expression(parsed.tree)
        if expression:
            print(f"Found expression: '{expression.raw}'")
            print(f"Expression type: {expression.type}")
            
            # Check what children it has
            if hasattr(expression, 'segments') and expression.segments:
                print(f"Expression has {len(expression.segments)} segments:")
                for i, seg in enumerate(expression.segments):
                    print(f"  {i}: {seg.type}: '{seg.raw}'")
                    if hasattr(seg, 'segments') and seg.segments:
                        for j, subseg in enumerate(seg.segments):
                            print(f"    {i}.{j}: {subseg.type}: '{subseg.raw}'")
            
            # Try to find select_statement
            select_stmt = get_child(expression, "select_statement")
            print(f"get_child(select_statement): {select_stmt}")
            
            bracketed_seg = get_child(expression, "bracketed")
            print(f"get_child(bracketed): {bracketed_seg}")
            if bracketed_seg:
                inner_select = get_child(bracketed_seg, "select_statement")
                print(f"get_child(bracketed).get_child(select_statement): {inner_select}")

if __name__ == "__main__":

"""
Test edge cases for subquery in VALUES clause
"""

from sqllineage.runner import LineageRunner

def test_multiple_subqueries_in_values():
    """Test multiple subqueries in VALUES clause"""
    sql = "INSERT INTO tab1 (col1, col2, col3) VALUES ((SELECT max(id) FROM tab2), 'text', (SELECT count(*) FROM tab3));"
    result = LineageRunner(sql=sql, dialect="ansi")
    print(f"SQL: {sql}")
    print(f"Source tables: {sorted([str(t) for t in result.source_tables])}")
    print(f"Target tables: {sorted([str(t) for t in result.target_tables])}")
    
    expected_sources = {"<default>.tab2", "<default>.tab3"}
    actual_sources = {str(t) for t in result.source_tables}
    expected_targets = {"<default>.tab1"}
    actual_targets = {str(t) for t in result.target_tables}
    
    assert actual_sources == expected_sources, f"Expected {expected_sources}, got {actual_sources}"
    assert actual_targets == expected_targets, f"Expected {expected_targets}, got {actual_targets}"
    print("✓ Multiple subqueries test passed\n")

def test_nested_subqueries_in_values():
    """Test nested subqueries in VALUES clause"""
    sql = "INSERT INTO tab1 (col1) VALUES ((SELECT max(id) FROM (SELECT id FROM tab2 WHERE active = 1) sub));"
    result = LineageRunner(sql=sql, dialect="ansi")
    print(f"SQL: {sql}")
    print(f"Source tables: {sorted([str(t) for t in result.source_tables])}")
    print(f"Target tables: {sorted([str(t) for t in result.target_tables])}")
    
    expected_sources = {"<default>.tab2"}
    actual_sources = {str(t) for t in result.source_tables}
    expected_targets = {"<default>.tab1"}
    actual_targets = {str(t) for t in result.target_tables}
    
    assert actual_sources == expected_sources, f"Expected {expected_sources}, got {actual_sources}"
    assert actual_targets == expected_targets, f"Expected {expected_targets}, got {actual_targets}"
    print("✓ Nested subqueries test passed\n")

def test_mixed_literals_and_subqueries():
    """Test mix of literals and subqueries"""
    sql = "INSERT INTO tab1 (col1, col2, col3) VALUES (123, (SELECT name FROM tab2), 'static_value');"

    print(f"Target tables: {sorted([str(t) for t in result.target_tables])}")

    expected_targets = {"<default>.tab1"}

    assert actual_targets == expected_targets, f"Expected {expected_targets}, got {actual_targets}"
    print("✓ Mixed literals and subqueries test passed\n")

    print(f"Target tables: {sorted([str(t) for t in result.target_tables])}")

     1	from sqlfluff.core.parser import BaseSegment
     2	
     3	from sqllineage.core.holders import SubQueryLineageHolder
     4	from sqllineage.core.models import Path
     5	from sqllineage.core.parser.sqlfluff.extractors.base import BaseExtractor
     6	from sqllineage.core.parser.sqlfluff.extractors.select import SelectExtractor
     7	from sqllineage.core.parser.sqlfluff.models import SqlFluffColumn, SqlFluffTable
     8	from sqllineage.core.parser.sqlfluff.utils import (
     9	    get_child,
    10	    is_negligible,
    11	    is_set_expression,
    12	    list_child_segments,
    13	)
    14	from sqllineage.utils.entities import AnalyzerContext
    15	from sqllineage.utils.helpers import escape_identifier_name
    16	
    17	
    18	class CreateInsertExtractor(BaseExtractor):
    19	    """
    20	    Create statement and Insert statement lineage extractor
    21	    """
    22	
    23	    SUPPORTED_STMT_TYPES = [
    24	        "create_table_statement",
    25	        "create_table_as_statement",
    26	        "create_view_statement",
    27	        "insert_statement",
    28	        "insert_overwrite_directory_hive_fmt_statement",
    29	    ]
    30	
    31	    def extract(
    32	        self,
    33	        statement: BaseSegment,
    34	        context: AnalyzerContext,
    35	    ) -> SubQueryLineageHolder:
    36	        holder = self._init_holder(context)
    37	        src_flag = tgt_flag = False
    38	        for segment in list_child_segments(statement):
    39	            if segment.type == "with_compound_statement":
    40	                holder |= self.delegate_to_cte(segment, holder)
    41	            elif segment.type == "bracketed" and any(
    42	                s.type == "with_compound_statement" for s in segment.segments
    43	            ):
    44	                for sgmt in segment.segments:
    45	                    if sgmt.type == "with_compound_statement":
    46	                        holder |= self.delegate_to_cte(segment, holder)
    47	            elif segment.type in ("select_statement", "set_expression"):
    48	                holder |= self.delegate_to_select(segment, holder)
    49	            elif segment.type == "bracketed" and (
    50	                self.list_subquery(segment) or is_set_expression(segment)
    51	            ):
    52	                # note regular subquery within SELECT statement is handled by SelectExtractor, this is only to handle
    53	                # top-level subquery in DML like: 1) create table foo as (subquery); 2) insert into foo (subquery)
    54	                # subquery here isn't added as read source, and it inherits DML-level write_columns if parsed
    55	                if subquery_segment := get_child(
    56	                    segment, "select_statement", "set_expression"
    57	                ):
    58	                    holder |= self.delegate_to_select(subquery_segment, holder)
    59	
    60	            elif segment.type == "values_clause":
    61	                # Process VALUES clause for subqueries in expressions
    62	                # Look for bracketed segments within the values_clause
    63	                for child in segment.segments:
    64	                    if child.type == "bracketed":
    65	                        # Process expressions within this bracketed VALUES segment
    66	                        direct_children = [seg for seg in child.segments if not is_negligible(seg)]
    67	                        for sub_segment in direct_children:
    68	                            if sub_segment.type == "expression":
    69	                                # Check if this expression contains a subquery
    70	                                # Pattern 1: Direct select_statement
    71	                                if select_stmt := get_child(sub_segment, "select_statement"):
    72	                                    holder |= self.delegate_to_select(select_stmt, holder)
    73	                                elif set_expr := get_child(sub_segment, "set_expression"):
    74	                                    holder |= self.delegate_to_select(set_expr, holder)
    75	                                # Pattern 2: Bracketed containing select_statement  
    76	                                elif bracketed_expr := get_child(sub_segment, "bracketed"):
    77	                                    # Handle nested bracketed expressions that might contain subqueries
    78	                                    if inner_select := get_child(bracketed_expr, "select_statement"):
    79	                                        holder |= self.delegate_to_select(inner_select, holder)
    80	                                    elif inner_set := get_child(bracketed_expr, "set_expression"):
    81	                                        holder |= self.delegate_to_select(inner_set, holder)
    82	                                    # Pattern 3: Bracketed containing expression containing select_statement
    83	                                    else:
    84	                                        inner_expr = get_child(bracketed_expr, "expression")
    85	                                        if inner_expr:
    86	                                            if inner_select := get_child(inner_expr, "select_statement"):
    87	                                                holder |= self.delegate_to_select(inner_select, holder)
    88	                                            elif inner_set := get_child(inner_expr, "set_expression"):
    89	                                                holder |= self.delegate_to_select(inner_set, holder)
    90	
    91	            elif segment.type == "bracketed":
    92	                # Handle the original column reference logic for non-VALUES contexts
    93	                sub_segments = list_child_segments(segment)
    94	                if all(
    95	                    sub_segment.type in ["column_reference", "column_definition"]
    96	                    for sub_segment in sub_segments
    97	                ):
    98	                    # target columns only apply to bracketed column_reference and column_definition
    99	                    columns = []
   100	                    for sub_segment in sub_segments:
   101	                        if sub_segment.type == "column_definition":
   102	                            sub_segment = get_child(sub_segment, "identifier")
   103	                        columns.append(SqlFluffColumn.of(sub_segment))
   104	                    holder.add_write_column(*columns)
   105	
   106	            elif segment.type == "keyword":
   107	                if segment.raw_upper in [
   108	                    "INTO",
   109	                    "OVERWRITE",
   110	                    "TABLE",
   111	                    "VIEW",
   112	                    "DIRECTORY",
   113	                ] or (
   114	                    tgt_flag is True and segment.raw_upper in ["IF", "NOT", "EXISTS"]
   115	                ):
   116	                    tgt_flag = True
   117	                elif segment.raw_upper in ["LIKE", "CLONE"]:
   118	                    src_flag = True
   119	                continue
   120	
   121	            if tgt_flag:
   122	                if segment.type in ["table_reference", "object_reference"]:
   123	                    write_obj = SqlFluffTable.of(segment)
   124	                    holder.add_write(write_obj)
   125	                elif segment.type == "literal":
   126	                    if segment.raw.isnumeric():
   127	                        # Special Handling for Spark Bucket Table DDL
   128	                        pass
   129	                    else:
   130	                        holder.add_write(Path(escape_identifier_name(segment.raw)))
   131	                tgt_flag = False
   132	            if src_flag:
   133	                if segment.type in ["table_reference", "object_reference"]:
   134	                    holder.add_read(SqlFluffTable.of(segment))
   135	                src_flag = False
   136	        return holder
   137	
   138	    def delegate_to_cte(
   139	        self, segment: BaseSegment, holder: SubQueryLineageHolder
   140	    ) -> SubQueryLineageHolder:
   141	        from .cte import CteExtractor
   142	
   143	        return self.delegate_to(
   144	            CteExtractor, segment, AnalyzerContext(cte=holder.cte, write=holder.write)
   145	        )
   146	
   147	    def delegate_to_select(
   148	        self,
   149	        segment: BaseSegment,
   150	        holder: SubQueryLineageHolder,
   151	    ) -> SubQueryLineageHolder:
   152	        return self.delegate_to(
   153	            SelectExtractor,
   154	            segment,
   155	            AnalyzerContext(
   156	                cte=holder.cte,
   157	                write=holder.write,
   158	                write_columns=holder.write_columns,
   159	            ),
   160	        )
   161
